Engineering Evidence

The machine-readable files and the Terraform draft.

Files in this report folder

Every file the run publishes, with what it is for. A name links to the file when it is in this folder and you opened this page from it. The size and the sha256 of every file are in manifest.json: this page is written before the manifest and a file cannot state its own size, so only the files of the Terraform draft show theirs here.

Present only when the register has more records than the page holds cards.Yes–
decisions.jsonThe decision register, machine-readable, with a template of the decisions file to fill in.Yes–
migration/decisions.csvThe decision records as rows for a spreadsheet.Yes–
executive/decisions.mdThe decision records in words.Yes–
executive/brief.mdThe five-minute answer as a document: the run summary, what holds the estate back, what people must decide, the shape of the target and what has and has not been established. It restates numbers this file holds and scores nothing.Yes–
migration/applications.csvOne row per application of the applications file you gave, with the values as you wrote them (a value you did not write is marked not provided), and one row per unit claimed by several applications. Header only without an applications file.Yes–
migration/waves.csvThe dependency order of the units, one row per unit: the wave, WITHHELD when a part of the source was not read in full, or REFUSED for a unit the partial draft refuses for a missing input (in no wave). An order of work, not a schedule.Yes–
migration/actions.csvThe register of what a person has to do, decide or establish before the draft is deployed, one typed row per item: gaps in the reading of the source, critical semantics left open, open decisions and manual actions of the plans, workload items that wait for evidence, and validation rows not yet established. Every member of decisions.json is a row; each row names the decision record that holds it. It lists what is open and decides nothing.Yes–
migration/work-board.csvThe Work Board as a sheet: one row per job of the migration, in one of four queues (provide information, approve a design, perform migration work, verify), with its owner, priority, what it waits for, what closes it, its evidence, the files it carries out and the steps of the execution plan it holds. Every step of the plan is in exactly one job, or listed as not taken; pages/work-board.html shows the same jobs. Header only when the run wrote no execution plan.Yes–
migration/source-target.csvEach source unit beside its STACKIT target, one row per unit: the target service and its resources, or in words that there is none, that the unit is refused or that the source switches it off, and how many of its settings are kept (PRESERVED), changed (TRANSFORMED), accepted (ACCEPTED_*) or still to do (everything else, including anything not known). Counted from the rows of assurance/equivalence-ledger.csv; a unit with no ledger row has four zeros, which says nothing was counted, not that nothing is left to do. Header only when the Workspace holds no counts.Yes–
migration/application-chain.csvThe drill-down from each application of the customer's file and each group the run suggested (PROPOSED: a reading of the source, not confirmed by anyone), one row per member unit: the AWS resource, the STACKIT target in the words of migration/source-target.csv, the ids of the decisions that still ask something about the unit, the steps of the execution plan that carry it with the number an answer leaves not taken, the Kubernetes manifest objects that tie to it by an exact literal match, and what the code scan says of the application. A run that read no plan or built no register marks those cells as not assessed, which is not an empty list. Header only when the Workspace holds none.Yes–
migration/prerequisites.mdFor each unit, the open decisions and manual actions that stand between its draft and its deployment, as its plan states them.Yes–
migration/data-movement.csvOne row per stateful source unit (a database, a cache, a volume, a file share, an object store): its source and target service, the data class, RPO and downtime tolerance you stated (or marked not provided), the provisioned capacity the source states (not the size of the data) and the cutover dependency. The mechanism of every unit is UNKNOWN: StackToIT never moves data.Yes–
migration/data-movement.mdThe same units in words, with what the source and the plans do and do not state about each. No size, change rate, duration, transfer rate or downtime is ever stated, and no mechanism is chosen.Yes–
migration/data-movement.jsonThe data_movement block of workspace.json with every stateful source unit (the facets, the unknowns, the provisioned capacity and where each fact is read from), as a file of its own, with the run it belongs to. workspace.json carries the tally of the units.Yes–
architecture/current.mmdThe units of the estate and the dependencies among them as a Mermaid flowchart, with the units of each application drawn together.Yes–
architecture/target.mmdThe same units with the STACKIT service proposed for each, its class and confidence, as a Mermaid flowchart.Yes–
architecture/delta.mmdThe same units with what the move changes for each: its class, whether it has a target, and how many properties the draft names as not carried.Yes–

architecture/target.svgThe drawing of the proposed STACKIT target of the Architecture section, as a file of its own.Yes–
architecture/delta.svgThe drawing of what the move changes, as a file of its own.Yes–
assurance/safety.mdWhat has and has not been established about the draft, row by row, with the global invariants. It approves nothing and asserts neither equivalence nor that the draft is safe to apply.Yes–
assurance/source-compatibility.mdHow much of the source was read, the source providers the assessment saw and the source types the knowledge base does not know.Yes–
assurance/target-readiness.mdHow the STACKIT targets of the units stand in the capability catalogue and in the pinned provider (maturity, lifecycle), and the readiness dimensions.Yes–
assurance/operational-readiness.mdThe workload readiness of the units (installation, source artifacts, target preconditions, cutover, day 2) and the support options STACKIT evidences. Terraform generated is not workload ready.Yes–
assurance/compliance-evidence.mdFor each STACKIT service of the draft and each framework in scope, the state of the provider's own evidence. It says nothing about the compliance of your workload.Yes–
assurance/compliance-evidence.csvThe cells of compliance-evidence.md as rows for a spreadsheet.Yes–
assurance/equivalence-ledger.csvThe equivalence ledger: one row per unit and semantic of the assessment that the source states (workspace.json counts those it does not state), and one row per unit and property for the properties the draft does not carry, no semantic accounts for or that are critical and still open. Each row says how the source states the value, what proves the disposition, where the draft carries it and what stays open. Dispositions are the words the assessment uses today; PRESERVED appears only where the assessment or the draft's plan states it.Yes–
assurance/evidence-ledger.csvThe evidence the ledger and the other blocks cite, one row per document, with what it backs, the date it was checked, the date after which it is to be checked again and its state. Evidence that states no such dates is UNDATED, never CURRENT.Yes–
assurance/critical-semantics.jsonThe open critical semantics, one row per unit and semantic, with the disposition the contract result gives, the cause code, the sentences that say why the semantic is open and what would close it, the instances of a repeated unit and the alarm card where there is one. These are the rows the decision records name as their members; workspace.json carries the tally of them, with the run it belongs to.Yes–
assurance/resilience.mdThe RTO and RPO you stated (or that none was stated) and, for each unit that holds state or states a zone, replication or backup fact, what the source states. No unit is called resilient and no intent is chosen.Yes–
assurance/resilience.jsonThe resilience block of workspace.json with every unit that states a failure-domain, replication or backup fact or holds state (the quoted source facts, the state and where each fact is read from), as a file of its own, with the run it belongs to. workspace.json carries the tally of the units.Yes–
assurance/identity-transition.mdFor each unit that runs as an identity or is one: the identities the source names, the static credential the knowledge base reads, the federation facts, the attachment the identity plan holds and its state. It makes no claim that federation is an option; privilege delta, break-glass access and human identity are not assessed.Yes–
assurance/sovereignty.mdThe sovereignty requirement (not provided unless your applications file states one), the dimensions of sovereignty with what the source shows and what the target is proposed to be, the dependencies and the exitability. Sovereignty is not residency; it holds no score and certifies no legal compliance.Yes–
assurance/sovereignty.jsonThe sovereignty block of workspace.json as a file of its own, with the run it belongs to.Yes–
financial/price-snapshot.jsonThe price_snapshot block of workspace.json as a file of its own, with the run it belongs to: which snapshot of the public STACKIT price list this run cites (source, retrieval date, sha256, currency, net list price) and how many of the drafted resources it priced. It holds list prices only: no quantity is read, no total is built, and no usage and no egress is computed.Yes–
financial/cost-model.jsonThe cost model of the run, line by line: for each component of the AWS source (EC2 instances and EBS volumes) and of the STACKIT target (servers and volumes) its quantity (a value, a range or unknown, and where it comes from), its price record or the reason there is none, its pricing state and its amount; then the totals of the estate and of each service for each side (full, partial or none), the comparison of source and target with the reason a saving is or is not given, the price sources (the local price cache) and the assumptions the figures rest on. An estimate under one labelled scenario, not a bill.Yes–
financial/priced-resources.csvOne row for each line of the cost model (financial/cost-model.json), source and target: the quantity with its provenance, the price record (SKU, rate, unit, the date it is valid from, the snapshot it comes from) or the state and the reason there is none, and the amount of a priced line. An empty amount is a line that is not priced, never a zero.Yes–
financial/mapping.csvThe technical mapping beside the prices: for each server, boot disk, disk and database whose source and target are both in the computed group of the cost model, what each side states about vCPU, memory, CPU model, disk capacity, IOPS, throughput and high availability. The source side is read from the attributes of the source resource, the target side from the catalogue snapshot of the region; a fact a side does not state is the words not stated. A machine type is one of the types of the catalogue with at least the vCPUs and memory of the source (a burstable source may be offered shared types); a storage class is the one the draft names (the customer's answer or the variable's default), shown with the catalogue's own IOPS and throughput. The table states sizes; it is not a statement about the performance of the target against the source (no benchmark was run, and a CPU model that a side does not state is shown as not stated). The last six columns add, for a disk, the fee of the selected performance class (the cost model's own amount) with the assumption behind the class, whether the class covers the IOPS and throughput of the source volume, and the smaller classes of the catalogue that still cover them with their fee and change (an alternative with the same or better figures than the source; not a recommendation), and, for a server, whether the flavor is with or without CPU overprovisioning (the documentation's terms, by the overcommit the catalogue states) and what STACKIT states of its lifecycle (a deprecated type says so, with the sentence of the documentation). The size match columns state the comparison of a server's machine type with the source instance and of a database's PostgreSQL Flex flavor with the RDS instance class (vCPUs, memory and the kind, single node or replica set, against Multi-AZ) as proven, to review, not met or unknown, with the sentence that says what was compared; a basis names a size only where the size match is proven.Yes–
financial/cost-coverage.mdWhat the cost model covers: the price sources it looked at, the lines by pricing state for each side, the totals with their state (full, partial or none), the comparison of source and target and why a saving is or is not given, the assumptions, and what the list prices of the STACKIT snapshot cover, with what is known of the terms of reuse of the price data.Yes–
engineering/not-carried.csvOne row per property the draft names as not carried: the unit, the node and the property, what the assessment states about it (the class and closure of the critical-property gate, the draft's own reason, the contract semantic that reads it, what closes that semantic while it is open) and the place of the node in the source. A cell the assessment states nothing for is empty.Yes–
engineering/not-carried-summary.csvThe units for which the draft names properties as not carried, with how many, and the units that have no count (their draft block was not accounted, or they need no resource of their own).Yes–
engineering/evidence-index.jsonThe reverse table of the evidence index: one entry per block of the draft, with the units it comes from, whether it is an HCL block, a comment or not placed, and its file and line, with the hop table and the checks over the draft. workspace.json carries the tally of the entries and, on each unit, the forward hops; this file is the mirror of them, with the run it belongs to.Yes–
engineering/trace/The full comment of each block of the Terraform draft that has one: the draft keeps a one-paragraph comment above the block (where it comes from, why its values are what they are, what is not carried, which decision is open) and a `# trace: TR-...` line, and the section of this folder that line names holds the whole comment the generators wrote, unabridged: the rule and knowledge-base stamp, the references with their digests, the evidence of each chosen value and the reason of each attribute that is not written. One file for each file of the draft that has such a block, named after it with the extension .md, one section for each block under an anchor that is its trace id; the blocks are linked to their file by `trace_file` and their anchor `trace_id` in engineering/evidence-index.json. This run wrote 8 files with 48 sections.Yes–
engineering/trace-index.csvOne row per block with a trace: the trace id, the block, the draft file, the source units, the lines of the comment as the generators wrote it and the lines the draft keeps, and the pointer of the block (the trace file and the anchor of its section).Yes11125

target-plan.jsonThe canonical TargetPlans the draft was generated from, machine-readable: one plan for each family of the draft that is written from a plan (the security groups of each source cloud, routing, identity, log sources, servers), each with how the draft wrote every resource of it (a resource, a comment only, or a statement about a block another generator writes). execution/The execution package: execution/README.md is the entry point for carrying the migration out (the verdict for the resources with a typed plan and the draft files it does not cover, what is created, what is not migrated, the decisions, the credentials, the order of the steps, the OpenTofu and API commands, the manual steps, the checks and the rollback); execution/terraform/apply.md, one request file and one script per STACKIT API call under execution/api/, execution/manual/actions.md and execution/verify/checks.md beside it. A rendering of execution-plan.json and of nothing else; nothing in it has been run, and a script stops before it calls anything when its step waits for a decision. migration/work-packages.csvOne row per work package of the manual steps of execution-plan.json for a spreadsheet: the steps of one phase, one owner role and one kind of work that one person carries out together, with their number, the resources they touch, the decisions they rest on and wait for, the strictest state among them, their success conditions and the ids of the steps. architecture/network-security.mmdThe network and security view as a Mermaid flowchart, or, in an estate with more than 120 units in it, the partition map of the files under architecture/partitions/ (one box for each partition, the arrows between partitions counted, every unknown edge accounted for). Drawn from the TargetPlans of the draft and the assessment: the internet, public IPs, load balancers, networks, subnets, servers, databases, security groups and their rules, network ACLs and routing. A path the sources do not state is a dashed edge labelled unknown with its reason, never a connection that was not read. Present only when the run produced the draft.Yes–
architecture/identity.mmdThe identity view as a Mermaid flowchart (an estate with more principals than a drawing keeps legible gets one sentence that names assurance/identity-transition.md, which lists every identity, instead of a drawing): the AWS principal, the decision the assessment and the plan hold for it, the STACKIT identity it moves to, the role assignment and its scope. A step the typed plan does not hold (the role assignment) is drawn as manual, and a step it leaves open is a dashed edge labelled unknown. Present only when the run produced the draft.Yes–
architecture/data-flows.mmdThe data flows as a Mermaid flowchart, or, in an estate with more than 120 units in it, the partition map of the files under architecture/partitions/ (one box for each partition, the arrows between partitions counted, every unknown edge accounted for): clients, load balancers, applications, databases, object storage and registries, with the protocol and port of each flow where the source states them, public or private, and the migration effect on the unit that carries it; what the source does not state is written unknown. The flows are the paths the listeners and security group rules permit, not observed traffic. Present only when the run produced the draft.Yes–
architecture/network-security.svgThe network and security view drawn offline: the same boxes and arrows as architecture/network-security.mmd, each arrow numbered, with a key that gives its two ends in the direction of the arrow and its words (protocol, port and direction of a rule; the attribute of a reference). Source links, target links (what a rule, route or listener of the draft permits), links an answer of the inputs file changed and unknowns are drawn apart and named in the legend. A view that is not drawn whole (a partition map above 120 units) is one sentence. Present only when the run produced the draft.Yes–
architecture/data-flows.svgThe data flow view drawn offline: the same boxes and arrows as architecture/data-flows.mmd, each flow numbered, with a key that gives its two ends in the direction of the flow, its protocol and port, public or private, and the migration effect. Source links, target links and unknowns are drawn apart and named in the legend. A view that is not drawn whole (a partition map above 120 units) is one sentence. Present only when the run produced the draft.Yes–
migration/roadmap.mdThe execution plan laid out in ten waves: wave 0 the decisions some step waits for, then the landing zone, network and security, data services, platform, workloads, data migration, cutover, verification and decommission. Each step names what it does, the AWS resource, the mode, what it depends on and whether it is ready, blocked by a decision, not ready or refused. It holds the steps of execution-plan.json only. Present only when the run produced the draft.Yes–
migration/landing-zone-requirements.mdWhat the STACKIT organization and project have to provide before the first apply: organization and project, network area and region, address ranges, routing, DNS, identity, service enablement, quotas to check, API access, Terraform permissions, external dependencies and the customer decisions. Read from the TargetPlans, the execution plan and the assessment; what they do not state is written as unknown and no limit or role name is invented. Present only when the run produced the draft.Yes–
engineering/explain-index.jsonWhere, in this folder's files, the entries and ledger rows of each unit sit, as byte offsets: the explain command (stacktoit explain) reads a unit's chain from it without parsing the whole files. Machine-only: it is not an exchange document and states nothing the files it points into do not state; without it, or when it does not fit the files, explain reads them in full and prints the same answer.Yes–
manifest.jsonThe run's identity (run id, source digest) and the list of the files it published, each with its sha256 and size; its status says whether the run is COMPLETE, PARTIAL or FAILED.Yes–

Evidence traces

Each drawer follows one resource from the source to its STACKIT target: source, evidence, resolution proof, semantic claim, intent, target capability, transformation, render, validation and the differences that remain. A step says what holds, what is open and where its machine artefact is. Nothing here is decided by this page. The expert view of a step lists the identifiers behind it.

  2. Evidence ✓ Established

    The generator that writes this unit's block states, above the block, why its values are what they are. These are the generator's own statements in the draft, not checks of the draft.

    • the credential of the clients of stackit_rabbitmq_instance.dependencies_mq: the instance has no host, port, user or password attribute (pinned provider).
    • host, hosts, port, username, password, uri, uris, management, http_api_uri and http_api_uris are computed attributes of this credential (password and uri are sensitive); the provider schema describes none of them.
    • A RabbitMQ credential is a user of the whole instance: the broker users of the source (and their tags and permissions) are not copied, and the vhosts, queues and exchanges of the broker are created in the instance outside Terraform.
    • STACKIT generates the password; the sensitive output dependencies_mq_rabbitmq_credential_password exposes it (a reference to this resource, never a value).
    • Read it after the apply and give it to every client before the cutover; the state holds it in plain text and the draft creates no secret for it.
    • DEFAULT plan_name: var.dependencies_mq_plan_name defaults to stackit-rabbitmq-8.16.50-replica (broker instance type mq.m5.large has 2 vCPU and 8 GiB (AWS price list, mq_broker_sizes.json), and the broker is a single instance: the smallest replica (three nodes, high availability) RabbitMQ plan of …

    • field: stackit_security_group.retail_app_ecs_ui_service_this.stateful = SAFE_OMISSION: the source group is stateful (AWS tracks connections) and a STACKIT group created without stateful is stateful: the attribute is not written
    • field: stackit_security_group.retail_app_ecs_ui_service_this.tags_all = SAFE_OMISSION: tags_all is the tags merged with the provider's default tags and is computed by the AWS provider; a STACKIT group has no such attribute, and the effective tags are written as its labels (the tags that are not labels are named on the statement tags)
    • field: stackit_security_group.retail_app_ecs_ui_service_this.timeouts = SAFE_OMISSION: NOT CARRIED when the source sets it: the timeouts of the AWS provider for creating and deleting the group; a STACKIT group has none, and they state nothing the group allows (NON_CRITICAL)

    In target-plan.json of this report.

    Expert: identifiers

    Artefact: REPORT_FILE target-plan.json /plans

    • decision: DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST (alias stackitroute:module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private:NAT_FIXED_EGRESS_ADDRESS_LOST)
    • manual action: alias stackitroute:precondition-outside-area:module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private (BEFORE_PILOT): Create the target project outside a Network Area: module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private is replaced by STACKIT's default routing, which was observed to give a routed network the same reachability only in a project outside a Network Area
    • no target: NOT_DRAFTED: STACKIT's default routing replaces the table, with a loss: a route of the table sends traffic to a NAT gateway (the others, if any, to an internet gateway), and the reachability is observed on the target for a project outside a Network Area, which this draft does not write. Observed in one sandbox project outside a Network Area (tested on a STACKIT project on 2026-10-10; routing tables are refused there): a routed network reaches the internet with no route and no routing table configured, a server without a public IP through the network's shared SNAT address and a server with a public IP through its own; nothing initiated from the internet reached a server behind the network address; egress is restricted by the egress rules of security groups, not by a route; routed networks reach each other with no table. No stackit_routing_table is drafted. A NAT gateway adds what an internet route does not. Its fixed egress address is LOST (observed): the address a routed STACKIT network leaves by is the network's publicIp, one address for every routed network of the project, not fixed (it changed twice when every network of the project was deleted) and not settable (a PATCH of it answers 202 and is ignored), so a third party's allow-list on the NAT gateway's address does not survive; a STACKIT public IP per server keeps its address, the only one the probe saw stay fixed. The egress restriction (the gateway as the only way out of a private subnet) is carried by the egress rules of security groups, not by a route (observed); whether the source restricts egress that way is for the architect. The absence of inbound paths of a private subnet is observed for a server without a public IP (tcp 22, 80 and 8080 on the network address). Not observed: a Network Area and its default routing table. The outbound address of the servers an allow-list names is the architect's choice: keep the changing address, or give each server that an allow-list names a STACKIT public IP
    • plan producer: stacktoit-stackitroute 0.1.0
